ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e3243	Good morning, Minister of State! The pilot projects that you have initiated are in every respect. My question: 162/M “Can you give us a brief overview of your three pilot projects in the field of national defence?”
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e3267	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e3283	Ladies and gentlemen! As you know, in the January of this year I ordered the implementation of three pilot projects, of course in accordance with the law. These are projects for the further professionalization of the Austrian federal army, also coordinated with the General Staff – regardless of the question of the military system. Coming to the individual projects, I can say that a very important project is the “voluntary military” project, because it strengthens the idea of militia in the Austrian German army. We also want to create experience with this project as to how the recruitment and deployment of our militia is given. This project comprises the pioneer workers in Lower Austria and in Salzburg with each 115 men. We already have 1,500 applications, i.e. a multiple of the target strength that we need to meet this project with life. This also shows that people accept these pilot projects. The second project concerns the reduction of functional data. We will carry out pilot properties in the Austrian federal army, six in the number to come out without ground defenses, and can thus ensure that these basic defenses change in the time of transition to the force. This makes more sense than being active in such functions as cooking, waiters or drivers. The third pilot project – I could only take a picture of myself a few days ago – is the professionalization of an association, namely the hunter battalion 25, which is set up as a model association. We will only commit professional and time soldiers there. This is also a requirement of the Federal Hero-Reform Commission, which has been borne by all the parties represented here in the House.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e7393	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! Mr Vilimsky, we have decided on the motion for a resolution within the Committee, where it has been made clear that, in autumn, the legal matter of asylum and foreign law will naturally be discussed and various innovations will be made. This time the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum is concerned with structural changes and not with legal capacity. You've got to keep that very close. However, I am still glad that this structural change will be made and that there will be a Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum in the future. On the one hand, this is a structural change which is very important for the further development of our very good asylum and foreign rights. But it is certainly also an important step in the field of administrative reform. With the establishment of the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum, competences and resources are bundled, double-tracks and overlaps are reduced, and significant administrative simplifications are being made. I think this is a very important step. I have already argued that it is in itself structural changes and that the legal matter is not affected. Of course, there are marginal changes also in the matter of law. If there are shifts in competence, it is of course necessary that the legal matter is also affected in one matter or another. So it's going to be 1. January 2014 will give a Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum, and all of the first instance's asyl and foreign interests will be dealt with there. In future, instead of the 194 authorities responsible for foreign law in Austria, there will be a Federal Office and nine national offices. And in the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum, all asylum matters are also dealt with, including the humane right to stay. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: The foreign police agents are at the Foreign Police! You should know that!]] – Listen to me, I'm still coming! Of course, one has to distinguish what the Federal Office is responsible for. The competences are very precisely divided between the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum on the one hand and the national security police departments on the other, and the district chief teams will continue to be responsible for the establishment and employment of foreigners. This division of competence is given. But one thing is certainly being brought together: All asylum issues in the first instance will be dealt with in the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum. That's what you can turn and turn, as you want. I think this is a very important step. This Federal Office will provide faster procedures in the future. If the procedures are done faster, there is faster legal certainty for those affected and all those seeking help. It will certainly increase quality. Best-trained officials will work in this sensitive area and further strengthen the expertise and experience they already have. If there are faster and more efficient procedures, then I am sure that there will also be a substantial cost savings in the future. This will not take place immediately, but we can imagine that there will be a reasonable, indeed huge cost-saving in this area. And in times of migration flows and scarce resources, the establishment of the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum puts a very important impetus or a proper step in the right direction. As legal advice has been repeatedly addressed in the Committee: In the field of asylum there are no changes in legal advice, in foreign law it is bundled in and for itself as well as the competences. ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e9299	Next speaker: Mr Eßl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e9321	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Human rights are fundamental rights, which are a central element in our society. On the other hand, the safety of the population is a high asset that must be protected. This is about the protection of property and property, but primarily about the protection of persons. Security is one of the most important prerequisites for a peaceful living together, for quality of life, for one to feel comfortable in a country or in a region. The police have a central role to play alongside other organisations. The police have to be contact person, conflict manager, consultant, also executioner of the laws as well as the guarantee of human rights and the protectionist of the population from crime. It designs practical safety around the clock. Respect for human rights has become much more important in recent years, and especially for the police sector is a human rights-sensitive area. The police and, above all, the woman's Minister for the Interior are aware of this to a very high degree. In the description of the professional image for the profession of a police officer or a police officer, the first priority is: ‘Human rights as a foundation. We protect and respect human rights. We create for all people the greatest possible confidence in their freedom and security. Human rights determine our entire action as well as the transition within the Federal Police at all levels. Since human rights are indivisible, they also apply to us.” Then it goes on to say: “We meet at all levels appreciatively, openly and with respect that we expect from others and that we bring to others in daily service.” The fact is, of course, that each act of office involves the personal freedom of another person. As a result, this particular challenge has been given to the police. In fact, human rights place these high demands on this police action because the police must act to protect human rights. However, it must not act outrageously or unlawfully, otherwise it could in turn violate human rights. Therefore, the human rights requirements for the police are not theoretical, but they are consistent with the standards of professional police work. Simply say: A professional police act at the same time acts in accordance with human rights. And because the protection of human rights is an essential task for the police, the Ministry of the Interior has launched the “Police Power Human Rights” project, which sets the target in 24 orientations. One central element in this is the dialogue, is communication. This dialogue, to improve this communication, is the call in this motion for a resolution, in which it is said that the dialogue and communication channels between the security executive and the various groups of society must be further developed and forced into a structured form, so that – and I expressly say so – the police can remain the largest human rights organisation we have in Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e10052	Mr President. High house! If we look at this motion for a resolution that the police should pay attention to human rights, then the question is what is the point of this motion for a resolution. There are two possibilities from my point of view. One possibility is that the government will once again make a resolution to pretend to be active to pretend to do something that is good for Austria, which is good for the police. The other variant is – and I believe that it already applies – that something is actually in the background here, which should be moved. Unfortunately, the motion for a resolution does not recognise too much, because at first glance, when we read here that human rights should be safeguarded or ‘police power of human rights’, we could say in principle: Good thing. But if you read the Committee's report, you really wonder why the police have to do human rights. Why? If you look at human rights, there is a lot to be done for the police if the police really want to do human rights. For example, there is the equal rights of men and women in human rights, the right to work and adequate remuneration, the right to an adequate standard of living, the right to education, the right to participate in cultural life and so on. And of course it is also the right to freedom, property and security of the person. Age-based means that young adults do not press the school bench with children, but they do their courses at adult education institutions or in schools that have already been involved in this field, such as externalist examinations. The examinations shall then be carried out either in one day or in partial audits before a Commission. The examinations in subjects such as German, English, Mathematics and Vocational Orientation are mandatory. ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e14238	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! High house! To chronology: Due to the painting in 2009, there was an order from the Salzburg Landtag to the Court of Auditors in March 2010. The audit of the Court of Auditors lasted from November 2010 to February 2011 and was planned for the years 2004 to 2010. The report was presented in 2012, discussed in the Court of Auditors and discussed in plenary. The purpose of the audit task was to measure the effectiveness, construction, contractual human and financial interaction of five organisations linked and active within the framework of the Festival, the internal control system, the development of expenditure and revenue, accounting and information technology. The Court of Auditors did not find any malversations, and at the same time, all the institutions responsible for implementing the recommendations of the Court of Auditors in the context of the Salzburg Festival have begun to implement them in parallel. What recommendations have been implemented very quickly? – There is now a new organisation of the company controlling, there is the annual accounts according to corporate law, a personal unbundling, more transparency and control and also the Corporate Governance Code is implemented. The request of the Greens, evaluation in the sense of corporate governance, is thus already fulfilled. In my opinion, the Greens' request is too late, would mean double work if we were once again to carry out an intensive evaluation of the Salzburg Festival and should therefore be rejected from our point of view.

[[18:03:30]] 14. Point Report of the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy on the Government template (1801 D.B._Other Organiser German law on the position of minimum stocks of petroleum and petroleum products (EBG 2012) (1873 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e18193	We get to the 14th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. We are going into the debate. I call Mr Haubner. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e18223	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President. Before I talk about the minimum stocks of oil and petroleum products, on behalf of our group, I would like to wish Professor Van der Bellen all the best for his future and to have deep respect for his work here in the House. Thank you many times and all the best! I sometimes wish that all the greens would be like you, but that's just a wish. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Now I come to the issue of oil supply law. The big headline on this oil supply law is security of supply, ladies and gentlemen. The oil supply was and is an important measure to meet current precautionary criteria. Nearly all emergency power units in Austria, whether in hospitals, in food distribution centres, in government offices or, of course, in military facilities, are operated with diesel, i.e. with a petroleum product. It is therefore up to the policy to provide for the emergency to remain operational as a state in crisis situations, especially in the case of power outages. At the latest since the first oil crisis, it is clear that security of supply in relation to petroleum products is limited only. The growing decline in oil reserves and world consumption of petroleum products increase the risk, increasing the likelihood of supply problems. In order to ensure supply, therefore, most countries have established strategic oil reserves that reduce dependence on oil imports, increase crisis resistance and compensate for fluctuations in supply. Strategic oil reserves are the strategic supply of oil, petrol, fuel oil and petroleum intermediate products. Following the first oil shock in 1973 and 1974, the oil storage company was founded on the basis of the International Energy Programme. These are the Austrian Supply Society for Oil and Oil Products, which secures our strategic oil reserves. The International Energy Programme obliges Member States to provide energy, to provide emergency services for 90 days. On this basis, the safeguarding of Austrian energy supply is based on our domestic energy supply law. In addition to the commitment made by the International Energy Programme, Austria is also obliged by accession to the European Union to keep minimum stocks in a quantity corresponding to the average requirement of 90 days of the previous calendar year. In 2011, Austria had an oil reserve of 2.9 million tonnes to ensure the supply of our country in crisis situations for 90 days. Our emergency reserves are stored, on the one hand, in the storage of the OMV and on the other, the RAG, which, with the search and promotion of oil and natural gas, have made a sustainable contribution to the energy supply in Austria for more than 75 years. Ladies and gentlemen! The supply of oil follows from a responsible foresight. It is therefore our duty to make this crisis prevention conscious and to restore sufficient oil for emergencies so that, in the event of disaster, the supply of Austria can be maintained for 90 days in the future. This law ensures this security of supply.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e18274	Next speaker: Mr Katzian. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00166_d7e18296	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! At the present more than hot temperatures, one does not want to believe that sometime again the cold can be brought back again and a winter can come, a similar to in February, which was called a record winter, in which the heating requirement has also been exceptionally high. Then the gas supplies from Russia went down, and there was the fear of experiencing a similar gas crisis as in 2006 or 2009. However, it has been so that, unlike in other European countries, the major problems have been avoided. In Austria, we have not really noticed anything about this development. We have not noticed anything either because we have provided in time and in the gas sector we have created the appropriate structures to cope with supply bottlenecks or failures for a certain period of time. With the availability of gas reserves in domestic storage, we are in the top European field and can thus ensure security of supply, especially for domestic customers. Of course, we do not only consume natural gas in Austria. We also consume oil, in addition to transport, for space heating and industrial applications. We are therefore talking about measures to ensure security of supply. In the field, as my previous speaker has said, we have been taking appropriate measures since the oil price shock of the 1970s. The then oil supply and reporting law in its original version with all the novels it gave in the meantime dates back to 1976. We are now taking a new law and integrating all these steps into the EU's oil supply directive. Nevertheless, only minor adjustments are necessary because we actually have a very good law and most of the requirements are already met. What's this about? – In essence, importers of oil, petroleum products and biofuels are obliged to form emergency reserves ensuring that at least the average domestic consumption of 61 days can be covered. This is a contribution to security of supply, and we thus ensure that oil is available accordingly even if a crisis occurs. We need this not only in the oil sector, we also need it in other areas. Since yesterday in the house there is the novelle of the Energy Steering Act, a matter which will occupy us in the near future, and last but not least, the Minister announced the Energy Efficiency Act in the Economic Committee, a major project. There is a great deal of unused potential, and it is about lifting these potentials together. - Thank you.

If one puts the security against this small extra extra charge in the balance sheet, security should prevail, and that is why this Economic Steering Act is to be adopted today. Thank you.

Instead, they often land in remote innhouses, where they expect a multi-hour product presentation. Angepries are offered here products such as crockery, blankets, mattresses or polsters, presented by perfectly trained, psychologically best trained sellers. Decorated with the most beautiful words and greatest promises, sometimes even evidenced by false statements, these sellers build up a certain pressure, and unfortunately some of this pressure has not grown and eventually buys something he does not need, and unfortunately, at often overpriced prices. In Austria, we generally have this market under control. It is because of the legal measures currently in force that such events have so far had to be notified to the Commercial Authority six weeks before they were implemented if they take place outside the commercial operator's premises. Unfortunately, in recent months it has been noted that this pass has been avoided, especially – and since the circle closes – in border areas, since such events have been advertised in Austria, consumers have been credited, often the potential participants have also been picked up in their home places or at collection points, but then suddenly the journey, as we are, has gone over the inn and then such an event has been held in border Bayern. With this innovation, however, we are closing this gap today, because in the future we will also have to report events that will be advertised domestically, but will then be carried out abroad. So at least the problem of the perceptibility of organisers is better dealt with than before. They are now accessible to the public authorities. Finally, thanks to my colleague, Konrad Steindlund, I should like to thank all the other negotiators who, in hard and lengthy negotiations, have reached a very good compromise with our Minister for Economic Affairs and who can present a tangible result. Thank you very much.
